<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-health-probe-detail-normalization"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator Health Probe Detail Normalization

### Root Problem

The Network tab coordinator health probe returned raw socket exception text for unreachable coordinators, while the worker-auth probe already normalized common reachability failures. That made coordinator status and URL-test feedback inconsistent and sometimes overly noisy.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\probe.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Normalized timeout/refused coordinator health failures to `Cannot reach <health-url>`.
- Returned structured HTTP status details for coordinator health HTTP errors.
- Bounded unknown coordinator health probe error text to the same short detail style used by worker-auth probes.
- Preserved successful health probe behavior and Network tab call sites.

### Validation

- Ran focused coordinator health/worker auth probe tests: 2 tests passed.
- Ran Python syntax checks for touched probe/test files.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 186 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 220 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 961 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This changes only failure-detail formatting for coordinator health probes; successful probes and worker-auth behavior are unchanged.

<a id="2026-05-10---worker-auth-probe-detail-normalization"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Worker Auth Probe Detail Normalization

### Root Problem

The worker-auth probe classified authorization failures by string-matching generic exception text. That could lose structured HTTP status information and made auth-test feedback less consistent with the coordinator health probe normalization.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\probe.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added explicit `HTTPError` handling for worker-auth probes.
- Preserved the existing `401 Unauthorized - token does not match coordinator` operator message while now carrying status code `401`.
- Normalized non-401 HTTP failures to `HTTP <code>`.
- Normalized timeout/refused reachability failures to `Cannot reach <coordinator-url>`.
- Preserved successful auth probes and Network tab call sites.

<a id="2026-05-10---http-json-error-body-preview-bounding"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - HTTP JSON Error Body Preview Bounding

### Root Problem

The shared worker/coordinator HTTP JSON client already bounded invalid success-body previews, but HTTP error bodies were decoded into diagnostics up to the full response cap. A large coordinator error response could flood worker/coordinator exception text and operator status paths.

- Kept URL and HTTP status context in raised `RuntimeError` messages.
- Preserved the existing unreadable-error-body fallback path.
- Preserved request construction, strict JSON parsing, and successful response behavior.

<a id="2026-05-10---worker-poll-auth-error-classification-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Worker Poll Auth Error Classification Hardening

### Root Problem

The worker poll loop treated any claim exception text containing `401` as a coordinator token mismatch. After HTTP error bodies became bounded but still visible, an unrelated coordinator error body mentioning `401` could produce a false auth-error status and unnecessary `claim_unauthorized` cluster event.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\worker.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added a small worker helper that recognizes structured HTTP 401 prefixes from the shared HTTP client and legacy `HTTP Error 401` exceptions.
- Replaced broad substring matching in the worker poll loop with the helper.
- Preserved the existing token-mismatch status and cluster event for real 401 failures.
- Kept timeout/refused and generic poll-error status behavior unchanged.

### Validation

- Ran Python syntax checks for touched worker/test files.
- Ran focused worker poll classification tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 189 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 223 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 964 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. This only narrows auth-error classification for claim failures; real HTTP 401 failures still use the same operator status and cluster-log path.

<a id="2026-05-10---worker-malformed-claim-diagnostic-bounding"></a>

A bad or noisy coordinator response could flood diagnostics even though the control-flow recovery was correct.

- Preserved malformed-claim release behavior and `/api/done` release payloads.
- Preserved existing cluster-log failure isolation for malformed-claim diagnostics.

- Routed coordinator URL tests, mDNS discovery results, coordinator queue auto-refresh/re-enable, coordinator status probes, worker auth probes, worker-board polling, and firewall check/add-rule callbacks through the helper.
- Added explicit fallback callbacks for paths that disable buttons or own visible operator status, with bounded fallback-failure diagnostics.
- Preserved normal successful scheduling behavior and existing coordinator/worker HTTP contracts.

<a id="2026-05-10---coordinator-state-transition-save-failure-propagation"></a>
## 2026-05-10 - Coordinator State-Transition Save-Failure Propagation

### Root Problem

Coordinator claim, release, done, shutdown, local claim, and stale-reaper paths already wrapped `InFlightRegistry.save()` so accepted in-memory state transitions could emit `inflight_save_failed` cluster diagnostics when durable persistence failed. The registry save helper itself logged atomic-write failures but swallowed them, which meant real filesystem write failures never reached those coordinator state-transition handlers.

### Files Changed

- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\registry.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_persistence.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Changed `InFlightRegistry.save()` to re-raise after logging save failures and attempting temporary-file cleanup.
- Updated the registry cleanup-failure test to assert the write failure propagates.
- Added coverage for a real atomic-write failure during an accepted coordinator done report, verifying the coordinator emits the existing `inflight_save_failed` cluster diagnostic.
- Preserved successful save behavior and the existing coordinator HTTP response contract.

### Validation

- Ran Python syntax checks for touched registry/test files.
- Ran focused persistence propagation tests: 3 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_network_persistence -q`: 205 tests passed.
- Ran network-focused aggregate suite: 242 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 983 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. Registry save failures now propagate as the coordinator already expected; all coordinator call sites currently catch and log them, but any future direct caller must handle save errors explicitly.

<a id="2026-05-11---tauri-close-readiness-warning-and-contract-gate-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-11 - Tauri Close-Readiness Warning And Contract Gate Hardening

### Root Problem

The Tauri shell checked `/api/backend/close-readiness` before native shutdown, but the unsafe-close confirmation only surfaced the backend state/reason fields. The backend already returns warning details that explain active-work blockers, and the WebView displays those warnings, so the native prompt could hide useful operator context. The Tauri startup route-contract diagnostic also did not include the expected auth requirement when a route check failed, making auth drift look like a missing-route failure.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri\src\lib.rs`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Extended the Tauri `CloseReadiness` payload model to accept backend `warnings` with `#[serde(default)]` for compatibility.
- Added bounded unsafe-close prompt detail formatting: up to 5 non-empty warning lines, each capped at 240 characters.
- Reused the same bounded-text helper for backend HTTP error-body previews.
- Updated the Tauri route-contract startup error to include `auth_required={auth_required}`.
- Added source-policy coverage for bounded close-readiness warning display and auth-aware contract diagnostics.

### Validation

- Ran `cargo fmt --check`.
- Ran `cargo check` for `apps\desktop\tauri\src-tauri`.
- Ran WebView static JavaScript syntax checks with `node --check`.
- Ran focused Tauri/API policy tests: 33 tests passed.
- Ran focused local API server/helper/static tests: 27 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 984 tests passed.

### Regression Risk

Low. The backend API contract and successful close behavior are unchanged. The native unsafe-close prompt now displays already-existing backend warning details with hard bounds, and the route-contract change only improves startup error detail.

<a id="2026-05-11---webview-command-feedback-warning-and-error-preservation"></a>

The local API command result contract and persisted `/api/commands` journal include bounded `warnings` and `errors`, but the WebView command history rendered only command, severity, and message. Some successful WebView command paths also wrapped backend command results in reduced objects before appending them to history, which dropped warning/error fields that operators need for blocked, partial, or warning-severity command outcomes.

- Marked warning-severity command rows as warning even when `ok` is true.
- Passed full backend command results into command history for completed-row open and rename-apply success paths.

<a id="2026-05-11---release-and-preview-script-gate-consolidation"></a>
## 2026-05-11 - Release And Preview Script Gate Consolidation

### Root Problem

The Tauri prereq checker and preview launcher did not share the same backend Python runtime discovery or local API import preflight. The release self-test also exposed that the bundled desktop Python runtime can run without the optional `zeroconf` package, while the mDNS module did not leave its optional dependency symbols patchable for diagnostics tests.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Prereqs.ps1`
- `apps\desktop\tauri\Launch-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-TauriPreview.ps1`
- `src\mediapipeline\desktop\network\mdns.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_network_mdns.py`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added shared-style backend Python runtime discovery to the Tauri prereq checker and preview launcher.
- Checked both `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe` and `ops\pipeline\runtime\Python\python.exe` before reporting a missing backend runtime.
- Made preview check-only validation run `mediapipeline.desktop.local_api_main --help` with the resolved backend runtime before reporting success.
- Included the selected backend Python path in preview diagnostics.
- Added explicit `None` placeholders for optional mDNS `zeroconf` symbols when the package is not installed, so tests and diagnostics can patch the module without requiring the optional dependency.

### Validation

- Ran PowerShell parser checks for the touched Tauri prereq and preview launcher scripts.
- Ran focused mDNS diagnostics tests with the regular Python runtime: 7 tests passed.
- Ran focused mDNS diagnostics tests with `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe`: 7 tests passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold -q`: 22 tests passed.

- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: 986 tests passed.
- Ran `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1 -SkipToolIntegration -SkipEndToEndSmoke`: layout, parser checks, Python syntax, desktop Python unit tests, environment verifier, and Tauri preview prerequisites passed; the run still fails at the existing reliability regression check for structured encoding/audio settings.

### Regression Risk

Low. The changes are limited to prereq/preflight script agreement and optional mDNS dependency import shape. Runtime mDNS behavior still raises `ZeroconfUnavailable` when `zeroconf` is absent, and successful preview launch behavior still uses the existing launcher path.

<a id="2026-05-11---structured-encodingaudio-settings-reliability-gate"></a>
## 2026-05-11 - Structured Encoding/Audio Settings Reliability Gate

### Root Problem

The release reliability gate still required a direct `flags_widget.configure(...)` call for the legacy `ExtraVideoFlags` control. The CustomTkinter settings controller had already moved this through a guarded `_configure_widget_state()` helper so widget-state failures are logged instead of escaping from settings refresh. That made the gate stale even though the settings behavior was correct.

- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Updated the reliability regression source gate to accept the guarded settings widget-state helper path.
- Preserved the existing requirement that raw video flags are disabled unless `EncodeTuningPreset` is `custom_legacy_flags`.
- Preserved the existing requirement that compatible audio codecs are disabled/reconciled unless `AudioPassthroughProfile` is `custom_codec_list`.
- Added focused tests for custom-mode enablement, preview preservation of custom raw values, preview reconciliation of structured choices, and warning text when a structured audio profile replaces a custom codec list.

Low. This repairs a stale source-policy check and broadens behavior coverage. Runtime settings save/preview behavior, raw flag reconciliation, audio profile reconciliation, and widget-state guarding remain unchanged.

## 2026-05-13 - WebView Completed Decision Detail Parity

### Root Problem

The WebView Completed page showed route, encoder, and audio/subtitle decision counts, but it did not show the route reason or a bounded preview of the audio/subtitle decisions already stored in the completed manifest. <a id="2026-05-13---webview-pending-publish-drain-action-confidence"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Pending Publish Drain Action Confidence

### Root Problem

Pending Publish had row diagnostics, blocker evidence, recovery dry-run output, command history, recent events, durable summary, and drain correlation, but the final action area still required the operator to mentally combine those signals before pressing Publish Parked Outputs. One existing read-only correlation path also filtered evidence wrapper rows as raw pending rows, which could hide do-not-drain evidence from correlation counts.

- Added a read-only Drain Action Confidence panel immediately before Publish Parked Outputs.
- Summarizes current parked rows, blocker evidence, validation checklist status, recovery dry-run status, latest drain command, durable drain summary, recent backend drain events, and selected-row evidence.
- Classifies confidence rows as blocked, review, unknown, or ready and gives concrete operator action text.
- Re-renders confidence after pending refresh, drain-command history refresh, and recovery-plan dry-run results so the handoff follows the latest loaded evidence.
- Corrected Pending Publish Drain Correlation evidence filters to use backend-authored evidence classes from `pendingEvidenceRows()` rather than passing evidence wrapper objects back through `pendingEvidenceClass()`.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-completedpending-publish-proof-polish"></a>

Completed and Pending Publish had a read-only output proof cross-check, but the common post-drain failure mode where Completed reports a missing output while Pending Publish is empty was still too easy to misread. An empty pending queue can mean nothing is parked, but it does not prove a completed file was successfully published or still exists at the final output path.

- Added `completedProofRowMissingOutput()` and a new proof signal for completed rows with missing output state and no exact Pending Publish or durable drain-summary proof.
- Completed Output Proof now shows those rows as blocked with a concrete action: open Completed Manifest, Pending Publish, Run Logs, and Last Stderr before rerun.
- Completed proof summary now includes `Missing completed output without pending/drain proof`.
- Cross-page context now adds explicit missing-output posture text and a next step when Completed has missing outputs while Pending Publish is empty.
- Kept exact path matches as proof, same-leaf matches advisory, and did not add repair, rerun, reconcile, cleanup, drain bypass, deletion, publish, or arbitrary path controls.

Low. This is read-only display logic over existing Completed/Pending payloads and durable drain summary data. The main risk is false alarm wording, so the text frames the condition as proof to investigate rather than an automatic rerun or repair instruction.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-live-verification-and-diagnostics-bridge-fix"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Live Verification And Diagnostics Bridge Fix

### Root Problem

The browser smoke pass found a real WebView refresh failure: shared diagnostics bridge helpers were stored on `window.mediaPipelineDiagnosticsBridge`, but downstream Launch, Queue, and command-review code also called `diagnosticsBridgeActions()` as a global helper. That made the full WebView refresh display `Refresh failed: diagnosticsBridgeActions is not defined` even though the backend routes were healthy. The same pass also showed the sidebar version text could briefly display the static V5 shell label before the backend bootstrap/snapshot product version arrived.

- `apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js`

- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Exported `diagnosticsBridgeActions` on `window` beside the other shared diagnostics bridge helpers.
- Added `renderBrandVersion()` so the WebView sidebar version uses the backend bootstrap version on `DOMContentLoaded`, before the first snapshot refresh.
- Kept the V5 preview shell/title separate from the backend product version and did not change `APP_VERSION`, sidecar versioning, route schemas, command contracts, or media policy.
- Browser-smoked Home, Launch, and Queue against a live local API server after the fix.

### Validation

- Ran focused WebView static route coverage: passed.
- Browser-smoked the local WebView: Home refresh succeeded, Launch Backend Preflight rendered, Queue Launch Decision Checklist rendered, and browser console logs were empty.

### Regression Risk

Low. The diagnostics bridge fix restores an expected global helper already used by the WebView; it does not add new actions. The brand-version change is display-only and intentionally keeps the backend product version authoritative.

<a id="2026-05-13---webview-release-gate-and-pending-drain-summary-fix"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - WebView Release Gate And Pending Drain Summary Fix

### Root Problem

The release verifier still checked only the WebView index, `app.js`, and `styles.css` as fixed files. That was too weak after the WebView was split into many daily-use modules, because a deployable package could miss a script referenced by `index.html` and still pass the fixed-file layout check. While exercising the release self-test, the broader reliability gate also exposed two existing drift issues: the pending-publish drain behavior harness did not load the new drain-summary helpers, and `Invoke-RetryPendingPushes` passed the pending drain summary item list through `@(...)`, which PowerShell 7 rejects for the typed enumerable parameter before the summary can be completed.

### Files Changed

- `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`
- `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`
- `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added `Test-WebStaticAssetReferences` to the release self-test.
- The new release gate verifies the WebView backend bootstrap placeholder, rejects unsafe `/assets/...` references, and checks every CSS/JS asset referenced by the static index exists in the package.
- Updated the pending drain reliability behavior extraction to include the pending drain summary helper functions used by `Invoke-RetryPendingPushes`.
- Fixed `Invoke-RetryPendingPushes` to pass the `System.Collections.Generic.List[object]` summary item list directly into `Complete-PendingDrainSummary` instead of wrapping it in `@(...)`.
- Updated the reliability config-preview fixture to include current required safety keys: `ValidExtensions` and `RobocopyFlags`.
- Added static regression coverage for the WebView asset release gate, pending drain helper extraction, pending drain summary call shape, and required config fixture keys.

### Validation

- Ran PowerShell parser checks for `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1`, `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`, and `Pipeline\Modules\PendingPush.ps1`: passed.
- Ran focused Python regression tests for the new ops/release/metadata/reliability assertions: passed.
- Ran `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-ReliabilityRegressionChecks.ps1`: passed.
- Ran `Test-MediaPipelineRemuxEncodeAIO-Release.ps1 -SkipToolIntegration -SkipEndToEndSmoke`: passed, including the new WebView static asset reference gate.
- Ran Tauri shell scaffold tests: passed.
- Ran `python -m unittest discover -s tests\python\desktop -q`: passed.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. The release verifier change is read-only validation. The pending drain change touches production PowerShell, but only removes an array wrapper around an existing list before calling an existing summary function; it does not change publish copy, reveal, deletion, manifest, sidecar, or retry semantics. The risk is limited to pending drain summary generation and is covered by the reliability regression gate.

<a id="2026-05-13---tauri-preview-launch-smoke-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-13 - Tauri Preview Launch Smoke Hardening

### Root Problem

The Tauri launch smoke checker proved the preview could open and close, but it could accidentally match a pre-existing `mediapipeline-tauri-shell` window/process and did not separately prove that the newly launched shell process exited after the close request. Failure messages also lacked bounded stdout/stderr tails, which made bootstrap, health, contract, and close-readiness failures harder to diagnose from CI-style output.

### Files Changed

- `apps\desktop\tauri\Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_tauri_shell_scaffold.py`
- `docs\TAURI_WEBVIEW_PARITY_MATRIX.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`
- `docs\V5_TAURI_TRANSITION_CURRENT_PLAN.md`

### Implementation

- Added baseline tracking for existing Tauri shell PIDs before launch.
- The checker now only treats newly created `mediapipeline-tauri-shell` processes as valid smoke-test windows.
- Added bounded stdout/stderr log tails to failure messages for early exit, missing window, missing backend, close-request refusal, and close timeout.
- Added an explicit close-request acceptance check.
- Added separate waits for newly launched Tauri shell PIDs and backend PIDs to disappear after window close.
- Added cleanup of new Tauri shell PIDs and new backend PIDs in `finally`.
- Kept the smoke path bounded and non-mutating: it opens the preview shell, validates existing backend bootstrap/health/contract behavior through the Rust shell, closes the window, and checks process cleanup.

### Validation

- Ran PowerShell parser check for `Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1`: passed.
- Ran focused and full Tauri scaffold tests: passed.
- Ran live Tauri/WebView2 launch smoke with `Test-TauriShell-Launch.ps1 -TimeoutSeconds 180 -CloseTimeoutSeconds 30`: passed.
- Confirmed no `mediapipeline-tauri-shell` or `mediapipeline.desktop.local_api_main` smoke-test processes remained afterward.

### Regression Risk

Low to medium. This is a test/checker hardening pass, not a runtime shell behavior change. The main risk is a stricter smoke checker surfacing real local environment flakiness; failures should now include enough bounded log context to diagnose them without leaving backend processes running.

The Rust Tauri shell had the right process-lifecycle boundaries, but several startup failures were still too generic for an operator or maintainer to diagnose quickly. <a id="2026-05-14---backend-media-policy-readiness-in-webview-settings-and-launch"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Backend Media Policy Readiness In WebView Settings And Launch

### Root Problem

The WebView Settings page had strong local builder cross-checks, but saved media-policy posture was still spread across Saved Settings Trust, frontend-only builder logic, Launch preflight rows, and backend risk summaries. <a id="2026-05-14---webview-settings-previewsave-result-handoff"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Settings Preview/Save Result Handoff

### Root Problem

The WebView Settings page had strong local patch impact, save readiness, save review, and launch-impact panels, but the backend Preview Patch and Save Patch results were still mostly visible as text detail and command history. Operators could stage JSON, preview it, then edit the patch again and still have to infer whether the displayed backend result matched the current staged patch. That left a daily-use trust gap around saved-versus-staged state.

### Implementation

- Added a read-only `Backend Preview / Save Result` panel to WebView Settings.
- Added stable patch signatures so the page can distinguish fresh backend preview/save evidence from stale evidence after the Changes JSON is edited.
- Added result rows for current staged patch, backend preview, preview risk output, explicit save-confirmation boundary, backend save, reload/saved-state proof, and mutation boundary.
- Strengthened Save Patch confirmation text so stale or missing matching Preview Patch evidence is explicitly disclosed before the backend save command is sent.
- Exported the new Settings preview/save handoff helpers for static coverage and future browser smoke use.

<a id="2026-05-14---webview-browser-smoke-runner-stability-sweep"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - WebView Browser Smoke Runner Stability Sweep

### Root Problem

The browser-backed WebView smokes shared CDP setup, but each smoke still owned its own `subprocess.run` result parsing and Chrome/Edge process cleanup. The generated Node runners also launched the browser with stdout/stderr pipes that were never drained, and then waited on an `exit` event after `kill()` even if the browser had already exited. That could make an otherwise useful smoke fail as a timeout or hang instead of reporting the actual WebView/CDP assertion.

### Implementation

- Added `run_node_browser_smoke` as the shared Python execution wrapper for browser-backed Node/CDP smoke runners.
- The wrapper converts timeout failures into the same bounded failure-reporting format as normal nonzero exits, validates a JSON object result line, and reports malformed/no-output runners with bounded stdout/stderr context.
- Added shared Node/CDP `launchBrowser` and `terminateBrowser` helpers.
- Chrome/Edge is now launched with browser stdout/stderr ignored instead of undrained pipes.
- Browser cleanup now handles already-exited browser processes and uses bounded wait/kill behavior rather than waiting forever on an exit event that may already have fired.
- Updated all browser-backed smoke modules to use the shared runner and browser lifecycle helpers.
- Added static scaffold coverage that prevents regression to per-smoke `subprocess.run`, undrained browser pipes, direct browser spawn, and unbounded `browser.once("exit")` waits.

- Ran all browser-backed WebView smokes in one unittest command: passed, 14 tests.

Low. This changes test/smoke infrastructure only. It does not change WebView runtime assets, backend API behavior, local API contracts, process-launch semantics, settings persistence, rename apply, pending publish, FFmpeg/media policy, Network lifecycle, Tk fallback, or V4. <a id="2026-05-14---browser-smoke-runner-windows-stability-hardening"></a>
## 2026-05-14 - Browser Smoke Runner Windows Stability Hardening

### Root Problem

The full browser-backed smoke group exposed a Windows-only harness failure where the Node/CDP runner exited with native crash code `3221226505` and no stdout/stderr, while the same diagnostics smoke passed when rerun alone. This made the validation ladder vulnerable to a nondiagnostic environmental crash after many headless browser launches, while still needing to preserve actionable WebView assertion failures.

### Files Changed

- `tests\python\desktop\webview_browser_smoke_support.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_browser_smoke_support.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_completed_pending_proof_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_diagnostics_handoff_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_high_risk_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_large_table_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_lifecycle_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_maintenance_reports_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_network_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_pending_drain_guard_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_rename_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_schedule_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_settings_launch_smoke.py`
- `tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_telemetry_smoke.py`
- `docs\BROWSER_SMOKE_FAILURE_TRIAGE_CHEATSHEET.md`
- `docs\BROWSER_SMOKE_TEST_RUNBOOK.md`
- `docs\TLDR.md`
- `docs\VALIDATION_LADDER_RUNBOOK.md`
- `docs\REMEDIATION_CHANGELOG.md`

### Implementation

- Added a narrow shared-runner retry for exactly the no-output native crash return codes `3221226505` and `-1073740791`.
- Added clearer CDP WebSocket open error text and extended the same single retry boundary to no-output CDP connection-open transients (`[object ErrorEvent]` / `CDP websocket error while opening`).
- Preserved normal failure behavior for actionable stdout/stderr, WebView assertion failures, timeouts, malformed JSON, browser console exceptions, and backend route-contract failures.
- Added focused unit coverage proving the shared runner retries once for the no-output native crash and CDP open transient, and does not retry a normal actionable failure.
- Hardened all browser-smoke temporary Chrome/Edge profile roots with `ignore_cleanup_errors=True` so Windows cache-file locks cannot mask the real assertion result during cleanup.
- Updated browser-smoke runbooks and triage notes to document the exact retry boundary.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-16---advanced-gate-persistent-topbar-toggle-hides-non-daily-driver-panels"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - Advanced Gate: Persistent Topbar Toggle Hides Non-Daily-Driver Panels

### Motivation

The operator dashboard has ~94 total panels across 13 pages. Only a subset is needed in a daily-driver session (health check → queue → launch → telemetry → accept completed → drain pending → rename → schedule/settings). The remaining ~59 panels — forensic evidence, advanced keys, diagnostics internals, network mode scaffolding, coverage reviews, etc. — add noise for experienced operators but remain available for auditing and troubleshooting. A persistent, low-friction toggle provides clean daily-driver mode without hiding anything permanently.

### What Changed

**`apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/styles.css`**

Added an `/* ===== ADVANCED GATE ===== */` block immediately before `/* ===== REDUCED MOTION ===== */`:

```css
[data-advanced] { display: none; }
.advanced-mode [data-advanced] { display: block; }

#advanced-toggle { transition: background 150ms ease, border-color 150ms ease, color 150ms ease; }
#advanced-toggle[aria-pressed="true"] {
  background: var(--blue-700);
  color: var(--grey-50);
  border-color: var(--blue-600);
}
#advanced-toggle[aria-pressed="true"]:hover {
  background: var(--blue-600);
  border-color: var(--blue-500);
}
```

The gate is purely CSS: `[data-advanced]` elements are hidden by default; `.advanced-mode` on `<body>` reveals them. `display: block` on the wrapper div is safe — inner element layout (flex, grid, etc.) is preserved because the property applies to the wrapper, not the children.

**`apps/desktop/webview/static/index.html`**

1. **Topbar button** — inserted between the close-readiness span and the existing refresh button:
   ```html
   <button type="button" id="advanced-toggle" class="secondary-button" aria-pressed="false">Advanced</button>
   ```

2. **Panel marking** — 43 `data-advanced` occurrences added across all 13 pages:

| Page | Marking strategy | Panels/groups gated |
|---|---|---|
| Home (Dashboard) | One `<div data-advanced>` wrapper around all 15 SV sub-panels (Conflict Summary through Session Handoff block) | 15 sub-panels |
| Queue | Two `<div data-advanced>` wrappers for sub-panel groups (Backend Launch Scope Preview, Backend Drain Scope Preview) | 2 groups |
| Completed (Output) | Eight `<div data-advanced>` wrappers (Run History, Output Proof, Output Verification, Evidence Packet, Route Agreement, Manifest Check, Output Checklist, Collision Risk+Excluded Files combined) | 8 groups |
| Pending (Publish) | Seven `<div data-advanced>` wrappers (size-growth sub-panels, conflict tables, coverage, final-trust, proof-review groups) | 7 groups |
| Rename | One `<div data-advanced>` wrapper (Outcome Review sub-panel) | 1 group |
| Launch | Seven `<div data-advanced>` wrappers (scope-reconciliation, pilot evidence, real-media proof, start-decision-summary sub-panel groups) | 7 groups |
| Reports | `data-advanced` attribute on full `<section class="panel">` (Pre-Launch Context section) | 1 section |
| Network (Workers) | `data-advanced` attribute on 4 full `<section class="panel">` elements (coordinator/worker topology, lifecycle, lifecycle-history) | 4 sections |
| Maintenance | No advanced panels — all content is daily-driver (toolchain health) | 0 |
| Diagnostics | `data-advanced` on 3 full sections + 5 `<div data-advanced>` wrappers (Page Handoff, Backend State, Open Files, API Contract sections; backend-detail sub-panel groups) | 8 items |
| Settings | `data-advanced` on 6 full sections (Advanced Keys, Advanced Key Plan, Safety Locks, Change Preview, Active Policy, Policy Readiness) | 6 sections |
| Schedule | No advanced panels — all schedule content is daily-driver | 0 |
| Telemetry (Live) | No advanced panels — live metrics are daily-driver | 0 |

**`apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/app.js`**

Added `initAdvancedToggle()` function before the `DOMContentLoaded` listener:

```javascript
function initAdvancedToggle() {
  const STORAGE_KEY = "mediapipeline-advanced-mode";
  const btn = byId("advanced-toggle");
  if (!btn) return;

  function applyAdvancedMode(on) {
    document.body.classList.toggle("advanced-mode", on);
    btn.setAttribute("aria-pressed", String(on));
    btn.dataset.state = on ? "on" : "off";
    try { localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, on ? "1" : "0"); } catch (_) {}
  }

  let stored = false;
  try { stored = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY) === "1"; } catch (_) {}
  applyAdvancedMode(stored);

  btn.addEventListener("click", () => {
    applyAdvancedMode(!document.body.classList.contains("advanced-mode"));
  });
}
```

Called `initAdvancedToggle()` inside `DOMContentLoaded` immediately after `initNavigation()`.

### Files Modified

| File | Change type |
|---|---|
| `styles.css` | CSS rule addition (`[data-advanced]`, `.advanced-mode [data-advanced]`, `#advanced-toggle` styling) |
| `index.html` | Topbar button insertion + 43 `data-advanced` attribute/wrapper additions across 13 pages |
| `app.js` | `initAdvancedToggle()` function + call site in `DOMContentLoaded` |
| `docs/WEBVIEW_DOM_ID_INVENTORY.md` | `advanced-toggle` row added to topbar section |

### Validation

- Full test suite: **1183 passed, 96 subtests passed**, 0 failed — `data-advanced` wrappers and section attributes are invisible to existing assertions.
- No existing `assertIn` call targets `data-advanced` text content, so no assertion drift from the gate.

### Regression Risk

Low. The gate is additive:
- CSS `[data-advanced] { display: none; }` only fires on elements with that attribute; all existing elements without it are unaffected.
- The topbar button is a new element — no existing ID collides with `advanced-toggle`.
- `initAdvancedToggle()` reads from `localStorage` on load; if the key is absent (first load), `stored` is `false` and advanced mode is off — correct default.
- Existing tests do not assert on `localStorage` state, `aria-pressed`, or `body` class names.
- Test suite result unchanged: 1183 passed, 0 failed.

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-11-missed-primary-button-empty-state-icon--copy-light-mode-css"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 11: Missed Primary Button, Empty State Icon + Copy, Light Mode CSS

Closed the four deferred items from Stages 2–7 that were not covered by prior stages, plus one missed button identified during the Stage 11 audit. CSS-only changes (Tasks 1 and 3) are invisible to the test suite; Task 0 and Task 2 touch `index.html` but do not change any DOM IDs, data attributes, or text visible to existing assertions.

### Task 0 — Missed Primary Button: Save Schedule

`schedule-editor-save-button` was incorrectly classed `danger-button`. Saving a schedule is a positive, reversible write — not destructive. Changed to `primary-button`.

- `index.html` `schedule-editor-save-button`: `class="danger-button"` → `class="primary-button"`
- Total `class="primary-button"` count: 3 → 4

Buttons intentionally left as `danger-button`: `pending-drain-button` (irreversible file move), `audit-start-button` (irreversible pipeline start), `rerun-start-button` (irreversible pipeline start), `backend-shutdown-button` (process termination).

### Task 1 — Empty State Icon (CSS `::before`)

Added a `::before` pseudo-element to `.empty-state` to provide a visual anchor above the label text. A pseudo-element avoids any DOM change.

```css
.empty-state::before {
  content: "—";
  display: block;
  font-size: var(--text-xl);
  font-weight: 300;
  color: var(--grey-600);
  margin-bottom: var(--space-3);
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
}
```

An em-dash at `text-xl` (24 px) reads as a visual separator rather than domain-specific content. Neutral enough for all table types (errors, files, jobs, events).

### Task 2 — Empty State Copy (Context-Specific)

Replaced the generic `"No items"` / `"Items will appear here when available."` copy on all 97 empty-state instances with context-appropriate wording. Instances fall into four categories:

| Category | Label | Hint |
|---|---|---|
| A — Backend-populated (refresh to load) | `No data loaded` | `Refresh to load data from the backend.` |
| B — Selection-driven (select a row) | `No row selected` | `Select a row above to see details here.` |
| C — Live-signal (empty = healthy) | Specific per-table (see below) | Specific per-table (see below) |
| D — Scope-preview (action required) | `No preview loaded` | Specific per-table (see below) |

Category C specific wording:

| Table | Label | Hint |
|---|---|---|
| Errors (Diagnostics) | `No errors recorded` | `Errors appear here when the backend reports a failure.` |
| Events (Diagnostics) | `No events recorded` | `Events appear here when the backend emits state changes.` |
| Flagged Items (Queue) | `Nothing flagged` | `Flagged rows appear here when the backend marks a file blocked.` |
| Flagged Items (Completed) | `Nothing flagged` | `Flagged rows appear here when output validation finds a problem.` |
| Flagged Items (Pending Publish) | `Nothing flagged` | `Flagged rows appear here when the drain guard blocks a publish.` |
| Collision Risk (Queue) | `No collisions detected` | `Collision rows appear here when two queue items would write the same output path.` |
| Excluded Files (Queue) | `No exclusions` | `Excluded rows appear here when files are filtered out of the active scope.` |
| Warnings (Reports) | `No warnings` | `Warnings appear here when the backend emits advisory-level signals.` |

Post-replacement verification: 0 occurrences of `"Items will appear here when available."` and 0 occurrences of `">No items<"` in `index.html`.

### Task 3 — Light Mode Token Set

Added two `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` blocks to `styles.css` immediately before `* { box-sizing: border-box; }`:

**Block 1 — `:root` overrides:**

```css
@media (prefers-color-scheme: light) {
  :root {
    color-scheme: light;
    --grey-900: hsl(220, 4%,  95%);   /* near-white surface */
    --grey-800: hsl(220, 5%,  90%);
    --grey-700: hsl(220, 6%,  82%);
    --grey-600: hsl(220, 7%,  70%);
    --grey-500: hsl(220, 8%,  55%);
    --grey-400: hsl(220, 9%,  42%);
    --grey-300: hsl(220, 10%, 30%);
    --grey-200: hsl(220, 11%, 20%);
    --grey-100: hsl(220, 13%, 12%);
    --grey-50:  hsl(220, 15%, 7%);    /* near-black text */
    --evidence-bg: var(--purple-100);
    --shadow-1: 0 1px 2px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.08);
    --shadow-2: 0 2px 4px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.10);
    --shadow-3: 0 4px 8px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.12), 0 1px 3px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.07);
    --shadow-4: 0 8px 24px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.14), 0 2px 6px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.09);
    --shadow-5: 0 16px 48px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.16), 0 4px 12px hsl(220 15% 10% / 0.10);
    --focus-ring:       0 0 0 2px hsl(215, 55%, 56%, 0.35);
    --focus-ring-error: 0 0 0 2px hsl(5,   68%, 48%, 0.35);
  }
}
```

Grey scale is inverted — dark-mode `--grey-900` (near-black) becomes light-mode `--grey-900` (near-white). All surface, text, and border tokens resolve correctly through the inversion without touching any class names. Mid-range status colours (blue, green, amber, red, purple) require no adjustment.

**Block 2 — `::selection` override:**

```css
@media (prefers-color-scheme: light) {
  ::selection {
    background: var(--blue-500);
    color: var(--grey-900);
  }
}
```

In dark mode, `::selection` uses `--blue-700` background and `--grey-50` text (near-black). In light mode, `--grey-50` becomes near-black, which has poor contrast against medium-dark blue. The override uses `--blue-500` (medium-bright blue) and `--grey-900` (near-white) — passes WCAG AA.

<a id="2026-05-16---v5-ui-stage-12-daily-driver-dashboard-rebuild--kill-backend"></a>
## 2026-05-16 - V5 UI Stage 12: Daily-Driver Dashboard Rebuild + Kill Backend

### Summary

Complete redesign of the WebView home page from an information-dense multi-panel layout into a minimalist daily-driver dashboard. Adds a hard-kill backend action. The advanced-mode gate (from Stage 11 Advanced Gate) wraps all existing home panels so nothing is lost — operators toggle into advanced mode when they need the detail.

### Tasks

#### Task 0 — Backend: Add Hard Kill Action

**Task 0A — `facade_process_control_policy.py`:**

Added `"kill"` to `PIPELINE_CONTROL_ACTIONS` and updated the error message:

```python
# Before:
PIPELINE_CONTROL_ACTIONS = frozenset({"pause", "stop", "rescan"})
PIPELINE_CONTROL_ACTION_ERROR = "Action must be pause, stop, or rescan."

# After:
PIPELINE_CONTROL_ACTIONS = frozenset({"pause", "stop", "rescan", "kill"})
PIPELINE_CONTROL_ACTION_ERROR = "Action must be pause, stop, rescan, or kill."
```

**Task 0B — `facade_process_control.py`:**

Added `elif normalized == "kill":` branch after the `elif normalized == "rescan":` block. Uses `kill_related_pipeline_processes(resolved)` (psutil name-matching; no process handle required):

```python
elif normalized == "kill":
    method = getattr(self.service, "kill_related_pipeline_processes", None)
    if not callable(method):
        raise RuntimeError("Kill control service is not available.")
    messages = method(resolved)
    message = (
        "; ".join(messages)
        if messages
        else "No active pipeline processes found to kill."
    )
    flag_path = None
```

#### Task 1 — CSS: Home Page Components

Added two new component classes in `styles.css` (after `.empty-state::before`, before `/* ===== PRIMARY BUTTON ===== */`):

`.home-start-result` — inline preflight result block; `[data-check-state="pass"]` green border, `[data-check-state="fail"]` red border.

`.home-compact-table` — 3-column compact table for recently completed; `max-width: 28ch` on cells with ellipsis overflow.

#### Task 2 — HTML: Home Page Rebuild

Replaced home page content (lines 51–716 of old index.html) with the daily-driver structure:

1. **Status strip** — 4 metric chips: Pipeline (`pipeline-state`, already existed), Queue (`queue-count`, already existed), Pending (`home-pending-count`), Role (`home-network-role`)
2. **Queue Snapshot panel** — `home-queue-snapshot-status` badge + `home-queue-snapshot` compact pre + "Full Queue →" cross-page link
3. **Run Controls panel** — Start (`home-pipeline-start-button`), Pause/Resume (`data-control-action="pause"`), Stop After Current (`data-control-action="stop"`), Hard Kill (`data-control-action="kill"`) + inline result block (`home-start-check-result`) + confirm row (`home-confirm-start-row`) with Confirm (`home-confirm-start-button`) / Cancel (`home-cancel-start-button`) buttons
4. **Quick Actions panel** — Publish Parked (`home-drain-button`), Schedule toggle (`home-schedule-toggle-button`), Review Pending cross-page link; note: `home-schedule-toggle-status`

6. **Advanced section** — `<div data-advanced>` wrapping all existing panels (System Readiness, Readiness Checklist, Current Run, Run Progress, Settings Health, Tool Status, Runtime Files, Recent Events, Recent Commands, Pipeline Overview + SV sub-panels); `processed-count` and `failed-count` preserved inside advanced section for test compatibility

#### Task 3 — launchView.js

Three edits:

1. Added `kill: "Hard Kill"` to `controlActionLabels`
2. Added `kill: "Force-kill the active pipeline process tree immediately? The current file will be aborted. This cannot be undone."` to `controlConfirmMessages`

### Summary

Restructured the Settings page from a single long scroll into 5 in-page tabs (Status, Editor, Media, System, Advanced). The Advanced tab absorbs the 5 panels that previously had `data-advanced` panel-level attributes, so those attributes are removed from the `<section>` tags. <a id="2026-05-17---v5-ui-stage-15-light-mode-toggle--colour-system"></a>
## 2026-05-17 - V5 UI Stage 15: Light Mode Toggle + Colour System

### Summary

Implemented a full light/dark mode system controlled by a persistent topbar toggle. Replaced the `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` approach with a JS-driven `body.light-mode` class so the operator can override the OS preference. Grey scale tokens are inverted in `body.light-mode`; status palette tokens are supplemented by per-component overrides that flip dark status backgrounds (visible only on dark surfaces) to light equivalents. Canvas chart backgrounds are theme-aware. Evidence panel accent colour switches purple → blue in light mode.

### Tasks

#### Task 1 — HTML: Theme toggle button

Added `<button type="button" id="theme-toggle" class="secondary-button">Light</button>` to `.topbar-actions`, between `#advanced-toggle` and `#refresh-button`.

#### Task 2 — app.js: `initThemeToggle()`

`initThemeToggle()` reads `localStorage` key `mediapipeline-theme` on load, falls back to `window.matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: light)')`, then calls `applyTheme(bool)` which toggles `body.light-mode` and updates button label. Click handler inverts current state. Called in `DOMContentLoaded` before `initSettingsTabNav()`.

#### Task 3 — styles.css: Light mode token overrides

Replaced `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` trigger with `body.light-mode {}` for both the grey-scale token block and `::selection`. Added 9 component fix categories (A–I):

| Category | Fix |
|---|---|
| A | Active nav button: `--blue-100` bg / `--blue-500` border |
| B | Danger button: `--red-100` bg / `--red-700` text (+ hover/active/disabled states) |
| C | Table status-row text: flip `--x-100` → `--x-700` for each status |
| D | Panel heading strong states: same flip |
| E | Selected table row: `--blue-800` → `--blue-100` bg |
| F | State pills: dark status bgs → `--x-100/--x-700` pairs |
| G | Topbar badges: same flip for ok/warning/updating/failed/safe/blocked |
| H | Home compact table dividers: `--grey-800` → `--grey-600` |
| I | Evidence panel accent: purple border → `--blue-600` |

Also changed `--evidence-bg` inside `body.light-mode {}` from `--purple-100` to `--blue-100`.

#### Task 4 — telemetryView.js: Canvas light mode

`drawTelemetryChart()` now detects `body.light-mode` class and branches chart background/grid/label colours. Dark-mode chart line colours (`#65a7ff` CPU, `#36d17d` GPU, `#ffc857` RAM) unchanged — vivid enough for both surfaces.

|---|---|
| `apps/desktop/webview/static/index.html` | Theme toggle button added to topbar |
| `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/app.js` | `initThemeToggle()` + DOMContentLoaded wiring |
| `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/styles.css` | `body.light-mode {}` token block; `::selection` override; 9 component fix categories |
| `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/telemetryView.js` | Canvas theme-aware colour branches |

<a id="2026-05-29---validation-state-contract"></a>
## 2026-05-29 - Validation State Contract

Completed UX-017 by adding a backend-authored `desktop_validation_state.v1` payload inside the existing read-only `/api/completed` response. The payload is built from completed manifest row evidence plus diagnostics output path/existence/health/size fields already loaded by the Completed preview. It reports row-level output path, output name, existence, size, probe proof, hash proof, playback requirement, validation status state, failure reason, unavailable proof reasons, and safe next action.

Completed Output Checklist, selected Completed row detail, and the Sample Validation completed-evidence handoff now surface whether a row is blocked, validation-needed, complete, or idle. Current rows explicitly report ffprobe/hash/playback proof as unavailable unless the backend provides those fields; the UI states that this checklist/packet does not run ffprobe, hash files, or mark playback accepted. This does not add a Local API route, command payload, command authority, repair/rerun/acceptance control, validation execution, output-integrity claim, settings persistence, media policy, queue mutation, pending publish drain behavior, rename apply, source/scratch/output media handling, Tauri lifecycle, or source deletion behavior. Validation: touched Python modules compiled; Completed JavaScript assets passed `node --check`; validation-state payload, completed policy/facade, Local API route workflow, WebView static, and row-detail smoke tests passed. Live local API browser refresh on port 11988 verified Output History showing `Expected validation contract: desktop_validation_state.v1`, selected-row proof gaps, and Dashboard advanced Sample Validation showing `2b. Validation state proof`; the temporary API process was stopped after verification.

<a id="2026-05-29---retry-state-contract"></a>
## 2026-05-29 - Retry State Contract

Completed UX-016 by adding a backend-authored `desktop_retry_state.v1` payload inside the existing read-only `/api/failures` response. The payload is built from failure JSON rows and failure marker evidence: `job_id`, source/stage/code, failure reason, retry count/limit, retryable classification, suggested action, and marker/report path evidence. It distinguishes transient failures that the backend will retry on the next queue pass from operator-required, permanent, retry-exhausted, or otherwise blocked rows that require operator review.

Reports > Failures now shows a Retry State column, retry-state summary copy, and selected-row retry detail with `retry_route_or_command` and safe next action. This does not add a retry button, route, command payload, command authority, marker-clear behavior change, queue mutation, process-control semantics, validation proof, output-integrity claim, settings persistence, media policy, pending publish drain behavior, rename apply, source/scratch/output media handling, Tauri lifecycle, or source deletion behavior. Validation: touched Python modules compiled; `reportsView.js` passed `node --check`; retry-state payload, failure preview facade, Reports facade, Local API route workflow, WebView static tests, and Reports browser smoke coverage passed before live browser verification.

<a id="2026-05-29---eta-telemetry-contract"></a>
## 2026-05-29 - ETA Telemetry Contract

Completed UX-015 by adding a backend-authored `desktop_eta.v1` payload to the existing read-only status and diagnostics DTOs. The payload uses only `desktop_worker_progress.v1` rows and estimates remaining current-stage time when a running worker row reports usable percent and elapsed seconds. If percent, elapsed time, or freshness evidence is missing, the contract returns an explicit unavailable reason instead of inventing an ETA.

Home Current Run, Progress Proof, and Diagnostics Runtime Progress now consume the ETA contract as proof-only telemetry. This does not add Local API routes, command payloads, command authority, process-control semantics, retry state, output validation proof, output-integrity claims, settings persistence, media policy, queue mutation, pending publish drain behavior, rename apply, source/scratch/output media handling, Tauri lifecycle, or source deletion behavior. <a id="2026-05-29---gpunvenc-usage-telemetry-contract"></a>
## 2026-05-29 - GPU/NVENC Usage Telemetry Contract

Completed UX-014 by adding a backend-authored `desktop_gpu_encoder_usage.v1` payload to the existing `/api/telemetry` DTO. The payload uses the cached telemetry sample to report GPU adapter/index, encoder utilization, memory used/total/percent, temperature, sample time, source, read error, active encoder count, and explicit missing encoder-session counts. Encoder session count stays `null` unless the sampler actually reports it.

The Telemetry page now includes the contract in readiness copy so operators can distinguish "NVENC utilization sampled" from "encoder sessions unknown." This is read-only telemetry. It does not add Local API routes, command payloads, command authority, process-control semantics, nvidia-smi query changes, encoder-policy changes, ETA calculation, FFmpeg progress parsing, retry state, output validation proof, settings persistence, media policy, queue mutation, pending publish drain behavior, rename apply, source/scratch/output media handling, Tauri lifecycle, or source deletion behavior. <a id="2026-05-20---layout-manager-movable-tabsubtab-boxes"></a>
## 2026-05-20 - Layout Manager Movable Tab/Subtab Boxes

### Problem

Customize mode only initialized direct page-level panels, so many Settings, Diagnostics, Completed, Launch, and Reports subtab/subsection summaries remained locked inside larger grouped boxes. Operators could move the parent panel, but could not rearrange those smaller daily-use boxes independently.

### Changes

- Extended `apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/app.js` so the layout manager initializes page containers, Settings/Diagnostics/Completed tab panes, and direct subsection bodies inside panels.
- Runtime layout setup now wraps loose tab-pane content and direct subheadings into generated `section.panel` boxes with their own panel keys, customize bars, and draggable handles.
- Drag ordering is scoped to the owning page/tab/subsection container so moving a box does not cross backend workflow boundaries or change command authority.
- Updated layout-manager CSS so nested/generated panels show customize outlines, and Settings-family inactive subtabs become visible while customize mode is active.
- Added `tests/python/desktop/test_webview_browser_layout_manager_smoke.py` and `ops/scripts/smoke/Test-WebViewBrowserLayoutManagerSmoke.ps1` to verify representative Queue, Completed, Settings, Diagnostics, Launch, and Reports boxes in a real Chrome/Edge browser.
- Fixed the stale Tauri backend-contract mirror so the existing backend-owned `POST /api/rename/browse` route is included in the shell's `/api/contract` validation gate.
- Updated smoke inventories, mutation-boundary docs, validation ladder, coverage matrix, Tauri README, and touch log. No backend route, command ownership, media policy, settings save, publish/drain, rename apply, source/output/scratch handling, or Tauri lifecycle behavior changed.

### Validation

- `node --check apps\desktop\webview\static\assets\app.js` - passed.
- `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe -m py_compile tests\python\desktop\test_webview_browser_layout_manager_smoke.py` - passed.
- `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_browser_layout_manager_smoke -q` - 1 browser-backed test OK.
- `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_browser_smoke_support DesktopApp.tests.test_tauri_shell_scaffold -q` - 86 tests OK.

### Residual Gates

The smoke proves real-browser customize handles and generated panel wrapping for representative tabs/subtabs. It does not prove a human's saved layout preferences across long-term browser storage or allow dragging boxes across unrelated pages.

<a id="2026-05-20---ui-improvement-backlog-closure"></a>
## 2026-05-20 - UI Improvement Backlog Closure

### Problem

`docs/ui/UI_IMPROVEMENT_CHECKLIST.md` still held 23 low-priority UI polish items that had become concrete operator-safety work: Launch controls needed clearer state, slow operations needed better progress/status, several Tk error call sites still bypassed structured `show_error()`, telemetry renderer failures could make a full WebView refresh look broken, and active docs still described the checklist as future backlog after the work was implemented.

### Changes

- Made Launch command buttons state-aware from backend snapshot and close-readiness evidence. Start/audit/rerun/drain disable while active backend work is reported, Pause/Resume and Stop become prominent only when relevant, and Force Stop is a hidden-until-active emergency topbar control that remains confirmation-gated.
- Added long-operation/destructive action tooltips, status-chip keyboard/title/aria hints, Rerun copy/keep/park policy tooltips, and Rename scrub/negative-term explanatory tooltips without changing backend policy ownership.
- Caught telemetry renderer failures as local command-history diagnostics instead of failing the whole refresh loop.
- Routed remaining direct Tk `messagebox.showerror` callers outside `widgets.show_error()` through structured error dialogs.
- Improved Queue refresh progress text with backend scan-detail fields when available, disabled Completed refresh/backfill controls while busy, and recorded CSV rerun failure root-cause tail lines in recent action history.
- Closed the active `UI_IMPROVEMENT_CHECKLIST.md` scope and updated active docs/inventories so future UI polish must be opened as a new scoped row in root `docs/OPEN_WORK_CHECKLIST.md`.

### Safety Boundary

This is UI/control-state and diagnostics polish only. It does not add or change Local API routes, backend command contracts, media routing, queue scope, source/scratch/output media handling, settings save semantics, rename apply behavior, pending publish drain semantics, network lifecycle, Tauri shell lifecycle, or V4/Tk fallback behavior. WebView code still displays backend state and sends backend-owned commands; it does not decide media policy.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-20---diagnostics-bridgeactions-refresh-regression"></a>
## 2026-05-20 - Diagnostics BridgeActions Refresh Regression

### Problem

The WebView top banner could show `Refresh failed: diagnosticsBridgeActions is not defined`. The immediate failing path was Reports diagnostics-action rendering: `reportsView.js` still called the transitional global `diagnosticsBridgeActions(...)`, but the bridge module only exposed that helper through `window.mediaPipelineDiagnosticsBridge` after later namespace cleanup. Older Launch history and command-review surfaces also still contain guarded global bridge-helper calls, so removing this specific compatibility alias was premature.

### Fix

`diagnosticsBridge.js` now restores `window.diagnosticsBridgeActions = diagnosticsBridgeActions` beside the other existing transitional diagnostics bridge aliases. The primary namespace object remains `window.mediaPipelineDiagnosticsBridge`, and new code should still prefer namespace-first access. `test_application_facade_web_static.py` now derives the diagnostics bridge helper names still consumed as legacy flat helpers and verifies each one has a matching `window.*` export, so future flat-export cleanup cannot reintroduce the refresh failure without first migrating the consumers. `WEBVIEW_GLOBAL_EXPORT_INVENTORY.md` was updated from 756 to 757 flat exports and the generated manifest now lists `diagnosticsBridgeActions`.

### Safety Boundary

This is a WebView compatibility/export fix only. It does not add or change Local API routes, diagnostics open/tail allowlists, command contracts, backend state reads, media policy, launch scope, queue planning, settings save, rename apply, pending publish drain, source/scratch/output file handling, Tauri lifecycle, or V4/Tk fallback behavior.

### Validation

- `node --check apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/diagnosticsBridge.js` passed.
- `node --check apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/reportsView.js` passed.
- `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade_web_static DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_inventory_docs DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_command_evidence_smoke -q` ran 17 tests OK.
- A Node harness loaded `diagnosticsBridge.js` and `reportsView.js` without pre-seeding `diagnosticsBridgeActions`, confirmed the helper was a function, and rendered two Reports diagnostics action children.
- `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\ops\pipeline\tests\Unit\Invoke-ActiveDocsReferenceChecks.ps1` passed.
- Final API recycle after the derived flat-helper guard: previous tokened API PID 12344 on port 11988 was stopped. New tokened API PID 29200 serves `http://127.0.0.1:11989` with token `codex-diagnostics-bridge-flat-helper-guard-20260520-verify`; health schema `desktop_backend_health.v1`, status ok, startup 14/14, close-readiness safe, contract schema `desktop_local_api_contract.v1`, 50 routes, queue schema `desktop_queue_preview.v1`, 123 rows, root HTTP 200 with bootstrap, `/assets/diagnosticsBridge.js` HTTP 200 with the restored flat alias and namespace object, `/assets/reportsView.js` HTTP 200, and `/assets/app.js` HTTP 200. <a id="2026-05-20---layout-manager-edge-cases"></a>
## 2026-05-20 - Layout Manager Edge Cases

### Problem

Two deferred WebView layout-manager edge cases remained open after the earlier customization work. A stored custom page order could keep placing newly added panels after every existing stored panel when the authored page schema changed, making new evidence panels easy to miss. Also, dragging an advanced-gated panel gave no immediate explanation that the panel was still gated and would not behave like an always-visible customized panel until Advanced was explicitly enabled.

### Fix

`app.js` now compares the stored panel order against the current direct-child panel-key set before applying a saved order. If the page schema changed, it preserves per-panel hidden/advanced state, resets the stored order to the authored default order, marks `data-layout-order-status="schema-reset"`, and leaves newly added panels in their authored position. Drag-start on an advanced-gated panel now shows a transient local hint in the customize bar: `Still gated. Click Advanced to make this panel always visible.` The hint styling lives in `styles.layout-manager.css`.

### Safety Boundary

This is WebView local layout-state behavior only. It does not add or change Local API routes, backend command contracts, media policy, launch scope, queue planning, settings save, rename apply, pending publish drain, source/scratch/output file handling, Tauri lifecycle, or V4/Tk fallback behavior.

### Validation

- `node --check apps/desktop/webview/static/assets/app.js` passed.
- `apps\desktop\runtime\Python\python.exe -m unittest DesktopApp.tests.test_application_facade_local_api.LocalApiServerTests.test_local_api_serves_read_only_web_prototype DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_css_design_tokens DesktopApp.tests.test_webview_inventory_docs -q` ran 10 tests OK.
- `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\ops\pipeline\tests\Unit\Invoke-ActiveDocsReferenceChecks.ps1` passed.
- Previous tokened API PID 22520 on port 11986 was stopped. New tokened API PID 7916 serves `http://127.0.0.1:11987` with token `codex-layout-manager-edge-cases-20260520-verify`; health schema `desktop_backend_health.v1`, status ok, startup 14/14, close-readiness safe, contract schema `desktop_local_api_contract.v1`, 50 routes, queue schema `desktop_queue_preview.v1`, 123 rows, root HTTP 200 with bootstrap, `/assets/app.js` HTTP 200 with `schema-reset` and the gated hint text, and `/assets/styles.layout-manager.css` HTTP 200 with `.layout-drag-hint`. The no-token listener on port 8765 was preserved.

## 2026-05-20 - Network Lifecycle Command Contract Gate

### Problem

The remaining Network checklist item described a real boundary: WebView Network had read-only persisted state and clear warning text, but no backend-published command contract describing exactly what future coordinator/worker lifecycle routes must prove before start/stop controls can be exposed. Without that contract, a future UI change could add a Network button from frontend intent before backend dry-run, cleanup, command history, close-readiness, and no-mutation proof existed.

### Changes

- Added `/api/contract` `network_lifecycle_summary` plus three design-only `network_lifecycle_contracts` for coordinator start, coordinator stop, and worker polling lifecycle.
- Added `docs/architecture/NETWORK_LIFECYCLE_COMMAND_CONTRACT.md` as the source-of-truth gate for future Network lifecycle work.
- Updated the Network page and Contract page to display the backend-published design-only lifecycle contract without adding `apiPost(...)`, Network lifecycle routes, or WebView lifecycle controls.
- Fixed read-only Network worker evidence so `heartbeat_age_seconds` is calculated by the backend from persisted `last_heartbeat` timestamps instead of always remaining `None`.
- Closed the root open-work Network item as a backend-contract gate. WebView Network remains read-only; actual lifecycle routes/buttons remain deferred until backend dry-run routes, command journaling, cleanup/orphan tests, browser no-mutation coverage, and inventory updates exist.
- No media policy, queue planning, source/scratch/output media, pending publish, settings save, rename, PowerShell pipeline execution, Tauri shell lifecycle, or V4/Tk fallback behavior changed.

<a id="2026-05-19---tauri-production-hardening-lifecycle-guardrails"></a>
## 2026-05-19 - Tauri Production-Hardening Lifecycle Guardrails

### Problem

The V5 Tauri/WebView2 preview shell had bounded backend spawn/bootstrap handling and backend-owned close-readiness, but the remaining production-hardening backlog still needed a visible WebView recovery surface for backend lifecycle events, explicit second-instance rejection before another backend could be spawned, and a focused audit for production devtools/token exposure.

### Changes

- Added a per-user Windows mutex guard so a second Tauri shell instance fails before backend startup, while non-Windows builds keep a no-op guard for source compatibility.
- Extended the Tauri backend process wrapper with explicit health-check and child-exit probes, then wired a bounded lifecycle monitor to emit `mediapipeline://backend-lifecycle` events for backend exit, repeated health failure, and monitor errors.
- Added `tauriLifecycleBridge.js` as an event-only WebView bridge. It listens through `window.__TAURI__.event.listen`, re-dispatches a DOM event, and does not call fetch, `apiPost`, shell/process/filesystem APIs, or backend mutation routes.
- Added a visible read-only lifecycle recovery banner in the WebView shell and token-based styling for the alert state.
- Added `Test-TauriShell-ProductionSurface.ps1` to audit dynamic window creation, static devtools flags, token-adjacent runtime logging, the single-instance guard, and read-only bridge posture.
- Updated Tauri/WebView static tests, mutation-boundary tests, global export inventory, lifecycle boundary docs, status docs, and the open-work checklist.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-19---adversarial-force-kill-encode-safety-smoke"></a>
## 2026-05-19 - Adversarial Force-Kill Encode Safety Smoke

### Problem

`docs/OPEN_WORK_CHECKLIST.md` and `docs/architecture/V5_MIGRATION_RISK_REGISTER.md` R-001 still had a runtime safety gap: no test force-killed the backend during an active encode and then proved a partial output was not accepted as complete. Close-readiness and UI lifecycle tests reduce normal operator-close risk, but they do not simulate an abnormal PowerShell/FFmpeg process death.

### Changes

- Added `ops\pipeline\tests\Invoke-AdversarialForceKillEncodeChecks.ps1`.
- The smoke creates generated media under `%TEMP%`, writes an isolated config, forces the generated source into CPU fallback encode, starts the real backend with `-Once`, waits for an `encode_temp_cpu_*.mkv` processing artifact, then force-kills the PowerShell/FFmpeg process tree.
- Post-kill assertions verify the temp output contains bytes, the source hash is unchanged, no Outsource file, local encoded file, completed manifest, or pending-publish artifact was accepted, and the same source remains in a backend-authored queue plan.
- Updated the risk register, test coverage matrix, test suite inventory, validation ladder, active checklist, and open-work checklist to record the new safety coverage.
- No production route, UI, publish, completed-manifest, pending-publish, source/scratch/output, or Tauri behavior changed.

### Validation

<a id="2026-05-19---rename-undo-state-root-safety-cleanup"></a>
## 2026-05-19 - Rename Undo State-Root Safety Cleanup

### What Changed

- Updated `src\mediapipeline\desktop\service_rename.py` so rename undo manifests prefer `State\RenameUndo` when the service is still using the legacy app-root `app_state.json` path.
- Added an app-root fallback so service instances with `app_root` but no resolved `app_state_path` write new undo manifests under `app_root\State\RenameUndo` instead of `%TEMP%`.
- Preserved the existing explicit-root behavior in `write_rename_undo_manifest()` and the existing temp fallback for bare helper/test usage without service state.
- Added regression coverage in `tests\python\desktop\test_rename_service.py` for the state-scoped path, legacy app-root path, and missing-app-state path.

The WebView remains an evidence, navigation, and command-surface layer; backend media policy, route decisions, queue planning, output acceptance, drain/publish decisions, sample-validation append handling, diagnostics file allowlists, and all file mutation boundaries remain backend-authored.
